# Front Binding of the Manuscript

The manuscript is encased in a robust and historically significant **binding** made of dark brown leather. The cover serves as the first impression of the work, decorated with a technique known as <term>blind-stamping</term> <note>A decorative method where heated metal tools are pressed into dampened leather to create deep, dark indentations without the use of gold or ink.</note>

The layout of the cover is structured by a large rectangular panel, framed by multiple parallel lines stamped into the leather. At each of the four corners, diagonal lines reach inward, creating a sophisticated "frame-within-a-frame" effect that directs the eye toward the center.

->**The Central Ornament**<-

At the very heart of the front cover is a prominent, vertically-oriented, almond-shaped motif known as a <term>mandorla</term> <note>From the Italian word for "almond," this shape was traditionally used in art to surround figures of particular importance, but here it serves as a central decorative anchor.</note> 

This central design is composed of:
*   Intricate, symmetrical **foliate scrollwork** (patterns resembling twisting vines and leaves).
*   Delicate **arabesque** <note>A style of ornament characterized by intertwining fluid lines inspired by plant forms.</note> patterns.
*   Four circular **rosettes** <note>Stamps shaped like stylized roses or stars.</note> arranged within the design, with two larger star-like rosettes positioned in the middle.

The spine, visible along the left edge, displays raised bands where the internal pages are sewn to the binding, showing the natural wear and character of a book that has been preserved through the centuries.

<summary>This page displays the front cover of the manuscript, featuring a dark brown leather binding decorated with intricate blind-stamped floral and geometric patterns. The craftsmanship indicates a high-status volume intended for long-term preservation.</summary>
<keywords>binding, leather, blind-stamped, mandorla, scrollwork, arabesque, rosette, craftsmanship</keywords>

# The Symbolic Bookplate

<meta>The handwritten letter "A" appears in the upper left corner of the pasted label, likely acting as a cataloging mark or a reference for the owner of the manuscript.</meta>

<insert>
A

On the inside of the cover, set against a background of marbled green and dark grey paper, is a rectangular label containing a circular alchemical emblem. This illustration acts as a visual summary of the manuscript's intellectual foundations.

->**Hermetic Philosophy**<-
<note>original Latin: "PHILOSOPHIA HERMETICA." This refers to a philosophical and esoteric tradition based on writings attributed to the mythical figure Hermes Trismegistus, which seeks to understand the hidden laws of nature and the cosmos.</note>

The emblem contains several layers of <term>Hermetic</term> <note>Relating to the mystical and alchemical teachings of Hermes Trismegistus.</note> and <term>alchemical</term> <note>The ancient practice of transforming base materials into noble ones, often used as a metaphor for spiritual purification.</note> symbols:

*   **The Ouroboros**: At the very top, a golden serpent consumes its own tail, forming a perfect circle. It is a symbol of eternity, the cycle of life and death, and the concept that "All is One." Golden rays of light shine downward from it.
*   **The Pelican in its Piety**: Centered within the blue oval is a white pelican perched in a nest. It is shown "in its piety," a term for the bird feeding its young with blood from its own breast. In this context, it represents the alchemist’s devotion and the process of sacrifice required for spiritual rebirth.
*   **The Crescent Moon**: The pelican’s nest is cradled by a silver crescent moon, symbolizing the "lunar" stage of the alchemical process, associated with the soul, silver, and intuition.
*   **The Rose-Cross Cube**: At the base sits a white cube—a symbol of the material world and stability. It features a <term>saltire</term> <note>An X-shaped cross.</note> with four red roses. This is a clear reference to **Rosicrucianism**, a secret society that blended Christian mysticism with alchemical science.
</insert>

<summary>This page illustrates a complex alchemical bookplate that introduces the reader to the manuscript's themes of spiritual transformation and ancient wisdom. It features iconic symbols such as the Ouroboros, the Pelican in its Piety, and the Rose-Cross.</summary>

<keywords>Hermetic Philosophy, alchemy, Ouroboros, Pelican in its piety, Rosicrucian, symbolism, bookplate, emblem, crescent moon, sacred geometry</keywords>

--- Page 11 ---
<meta>This page serves as a frontispiece or symbolic illustration following the title page of the 1788 Rosicrucian manuscript. It visually introduces the core principles of their alchemical philosophy before the letters begin.</meta>

### [Symbolic Illustration]
## The Philosophical Tree of the Three Primes

->An illustration depicting the growth of the alchemical work from its foundational elements.<-

The image features a <term>Philosophical Tree: In alchemy, a tree represents the organic growth of a chemical process and the maturation of the "Stone" or the "Universal" medicine.</term> The tree emerges from a base of three textured stones, symbolizing the <term>Lapis: The Latin term for "Stone," here referring to the physical matter or the "First Matter" from which the work begins.</term>

The tree contains seven distinct clusters of leaves. Each cluster is divided into three separate leaves, each marked with a symbol for the **Tria Prima**:

*   **Mercury** (☿) — Positioned at the top of every cluster; it represents the spirit, the fluid principle, and volatility.
*   **Sulfur** (🜍) — Positioned on the bottom-left of every cluster; it represents the soul, the oily principle, and the power of combustion.
*   **Salt** (🜔) — Positioned on the bottom-right of every cluster; it represents the body, the earth principle, and physical stability.

<note>The repetition of these three symbols seven times likely refers to the "Seven Stages" of alchemical transformation or the seven classical metals (Gold, Silver, Mercury, Copper, Iron, Tin, and Lead) being brought into harmony through these three fundamental components.</note>

---

### Key Concepts

*   **Alchemy** — The ancient art of transformation, aiming to perfect both matter and the human soul.
*   **Tria Prima** — <note>original: "Tria Prima"</note> The "Three Primes" or three first principles (Mercury, Sulfur, and Salt) defined by the physician Paracelsus as the building blocks of all things.
*   **Mercury, Sulfur, and Salt** — The spiritual, soulful, and physical components of any substance.

<summary>This illustration depicts the "Philosophical Tree" growing from three stones, representing the alchemical process. The tree's leaves are labeled with the symbols for Mercury, Sulfur, and Salt, highlighting the Rosicrucian focus on the Three Primes as the foundation of the Great Work.</summary>
<keywords>Philosophical Tree, Tria Prima, Mercury, Sulfur, Salt, Lapis, Alchemical Symbols, Rosicrucianism, Seven Stages</keywords>

### Alchemical Exclamations

<term>Trifolium</term><note>original: "Trifolium" — Latin for "three-leaved," referring to clover or trefoil. In this context, it serves as an alchemical metaphor for the "Three-in-One" nature of matter.</note>, a little herb right here
You find with me on the green heath,
Which with three little leaves
Is finely adorned by Nature:
From it separate and distil <note>original: "brenn" — literally "burn," but in this context refers to distillation or the application of laboratory heat.</note> a clear water,
Following the host of wise masters,
And then seek Three from One in total,
Salt, Sulfur, and Mercury <note>These are the "Tria Prima" or Three Primes, the foundational elements of Paracelsian alchemy.</note>.
Put these back together well
And <term>coagulate</term><note>In alchemy, the process of "coagulation" refers to solidifying a substance or bringing it into a physical, fixed form.</note> them on one stem;
Thus you will obtain this herb's juice,
Its virtue, strength, and great power—
A medicine in times of sickness,
A benefit for humans and metals <note>This refers to the "Universal Medicine" or Philosopher's Stone, believed to heal human illness and "cure" base metals by turning them into gold.</note>.

->†<-

## First Letter.

In the year 1722, I received this letter from Prince Utasop, otherwise called Friedrich Gualdianus <note>Friedrich Gualdus (or Gualdianus) was a legendary figure in 17th and 18th-century alchemy, rumored to be a Rosicrucian who had discovered the elixir of life and lived for centuries.</note>, which reads as follows:

My son, there is only one single matter <note>original: "Materia"</note> in nature, known to so few, and yet it is to be found everywhere; this matter is called by many different names: sometimes it is the <term>Saturn</term> <note>original: "♄." In alchemical notation, the symbol for the planet Saturn represents the metal lead.</note> of the Ancients, sometimes their *immature electrum* <note>original: "Electrum immaturum." A term often used to describe a natural metallic alloy or an unrefined ore containing the "seed" of gold.</note>, sometimes their gray wolf <note>original: "grauer Wolf." An alchemical metaphor usually referring to antimony, known for its ability to "devour" or purify other metals in a crucible.</note>, or their old gray-bearded man; however, among these countless names, the matter is recognized by few, unless the seeking devotee <note>original: "Liebhaber." Literally "lover" of the art; a common term for a practicing alchemist.</note> has a knowledge of mining <note>original: "bergmännisch." This suggests that the substance is a raw mineral or ore as it is found in the earth.</note>, then he finds it easily. For it is a thing driven together <note>original: "zusammengetriebenes Ding." Referring to a substance concentrated or composed by natural geological forces.</note>, which nature [leaves] imperfect

<summary>This page introduces a letter from the legendary alchemist Friedrich Gualdianus, identifying the "First Matter" of alchemy as a common but misunderstood mineral known by many symbolic names like Saturn or the Gray Wolf.</summary>

--- Page 21 ---
<meta>This page continues the instructions for treating the "matter" (crushed coal-related mineral). ## Second Letter.

In the year 1722, the following work was sent to me from Utrecht by Friedrich Stein, acting as the **Emperor of the Fraternity of the Rosy and Golden Cross** <note>original Latin: Imperatore Fraternitatis Roseae et Aureae Crucis. This refers to the Gold- und Rosenkreuzer, a prominent alchemical and mystical secret society.</note>.

->My Son,<-

> After I, for a long time with sorrowful worries,
> Traveled through many lands, from evening toward morning <note>Meaning from West to East.</note>,

> Everyone tormented me, and could greatly mock me
> In the showing of this way; here I was laughed at,
> There sent further along, and made a fool of here.

Until I finally considered within myself that whoever truly possessed the **Art** <note>The "Art" refers to Alchemy—the secret knowledge of transmuting metals and achieving spiritual perfection.</note> would not offer it to me for money, and whoever did not understand it could not tell it to me anyway; until I finally came to an old, good friend after many travels. This man did not speak like the previous ones, who were nothing but conceited sophists and "all-knowing" <term>chemists</term> <note>original: Chymici; at this time, "chemist" and "alchemist" were often used interchangeably, though the author uses it here to describe those who claim knowledge they do not possess.</note>, whom I had nonetheless often idolized and who, in addition, cost me much money

> The water encloses it, yet on this gray earth,
> If you labor diligently, it may well become your lot.

Through this, you can learn to rightly recognize the <term>raw matter</term> <note>original Latin: Materiam crudam. This is the "First Matter" that has not yet been processed by the alchemist's fire.</note>:

> Sometimes it looks spherical, sometimes oblong, sometimes squared;
> A star-white luster adorns the black coal;

That is, our matter is found within black <term>pit-coal</term> <note>original: Steinkohlen. The author suggests that the secret mineral is often found as an inclusion or ore near coal deposits.</note>; it then appears spherical, sometimes oblong, or squared; they give off a luster in the light, for they are coated like <term>gold</term> <note>original symbol: ☉, referring to Sol or the Sun.</note>; therefore it is said: "a star-white luster adorns the black coal." Regarding this matter, it further says:

> In these you find the elements joined together,
> From which all things, even the metals, originate.

Furthermore, I will name it for you: it is called <term>marcasite</term> <note>original: Marcasit</note>, <term>pyrite</term> <note>original symbol: ♃ (Jupiter/Tin), used here to denote "Kieß" or mineral ore</note>; I tell you, it is a <term>salt</term> <note>original symbol: 🜔</note>, a salt that no one knows because it does not look like salt. For when our <term>matter</term> <note>original: Materie</note> is first found, it is so hard that one can strike <term>fire</term> <note>original symbol: △</note> with it. But when it lies in the <term>fire</term> <note>original symbol: △</note>, it becomes alive, attracts its <term>virtue</term> <note>original Latin: virtutem, meaning its inherent power or spiritual essence</note> from the air, and becomes salty and <term>vitriolic</term> <note>original symbol: ⊕, the symbol for vitriol or metallic sulfates</note>, yet it is not common salt; therefore it is said: it is the condensed essence of the first "Nothing"; a smoke from the smoke. It is called a <term>Hyle</term> <note>from the Greek word for "matter" or "raw material"</note> and <term>Chaos</term> <note>referring to the unformed primordial state of the substance</note> is its name; for when it has become <term>Saturn</term> <note>original text: ♄no (Saturno). In alchemy, Saturn usually represents lead or the heavy, "dead" state of matter</note>, who now carries a child in his body, and when this has been ripened after a preceding <term>putrefaction</term> <note>the process of rot or chemical breakdown used to "kill" the old form of a metal</note> (which occurs in a moist, steaming heat); then it is carefully driven out seven times through a gentle <term>cohobation</term> <note>the process of repeatedly distilling a liquid and pouring it back over the solid residue to concentrate its power</note>, appearing as a watery <term>fire</term> <note>original symbol: △</note> and a fiery <term>water</term> <note>original symbol: ▽</note> seed—though the fire-seed should not be driven up before the fourth or sixth cohobation. For the watery <term>fire</term> <note>original symbol: △</note> must gradually loosen the remaining <term>water</term> <note>original symbol: ▽</note> seed from the bonds of its body. Therefore it is said once more:

> From this alone the Stone of the Wise is cooked,
> Which nature otherwise has never been able to produce.

### A Brief Recapitulation of the Whole Work.

After it has lain for its time in the grave

## Third Letter.

In the year <note>original: Anno</note> 1723, for the purpose of the <term>Manipulation</term> <note>the practical chemical handling of substances</note>, it was secretly revealed to me by the Baron von Rieth, a fellow brother of the <term>Society of the Rose Cross</term> <note>original: societatis Roseae crucis; the Rosicrucians, a legendary secret society of alchemists and mystics</note>:

The brother should know that from the beginning of the world, the Sages have possessed only one single <term>matter</term> <note>the raw starting material for the Great Work, often described as something common and overlooked</note>, which they kept so secret that they thought only of how they might hide it from the conceited people of this world. We wish, therefore, to reveal it to the brother through a <term>Philosophers' Rhythm</term> <note>original: Rythmo philosophorum; a mnemonic verse or riddle</note>. 1) Regarding the matter, it speaks thus as our <term>Saturn</term> <note>original: ♄; the planetary and alchemical symbol for Saturn or lead, often representing the "First Matter" in its dark, unrefined state</note>:

> I am the worst of things, yet also the very
> ->best,<-
> Therefore many strange guests find their way to me
> And seek liquid honey <note>original: Honigseim; represents the sweet, spiritual essence hidden within the bitter raw material</note>, yet they stand frozen in awe
> And take offense only at my black beard.

Here the form of the matter is indicated, and how many are offended by it <note>a reference to the *nigredo* or "blackening" phase of alchemy, where the substance looks like worthless black sludge, causing many to abandon the work</note>.

It emphasizes that the true material of the Sages is often rejected by the ignorant due to its lowly appearance.</summary>

# Fourth Letter.

<image-desc>A decorative woodcut ornament with a horizontal bar and stylized leaf or scroll flourishes at the ends.</image-desc>

In the year 1723, the following was given to me for manipulation by the Lord of Rittersbach, a fellow brother of the **Fraternity of the Rosy Cross** <note>original: *Fraternitatis Roseae Crucis*; the Rosicrucians, a secret society of mystics and alchemists</note>, and it reads as follows:

Dear Brother, there are many people in the world who have ventured to seek this great mystery, and have lost their property and goods over it. The reason for this is because the **Philosophers** <note>Alchemists often referred to themselves as "Philosophers"</note> concealed their own and only **Materia** <term>Materia: The "First Matter" or starting substance required to begin the Great Work</term>; therefore, they have fallen upon various subjects: sometimes upon **Antimony** <term><note>Antimony symbol</note>♁</term>, sometimes upon **Gold** <term><note>Gold symbol</note>☉</term>, **Lead** <term><note>Lead symbol</note>♄</term>, ore, bismuth, **Sulfur** <term><note>Sulfur symbol</note>🜍</term>, calamine <note>a zinc ore</note>, bloodstone <note>hematite</note>, upon grey magnesia, **Sea Salt** <term><note>Salt symbol</note>⊖</term>, **Sal Ammoniac** <note>Square alchemical symbol ◻; a salt derived from organic matter or soot</note>, and blood, saliva, and dung; sometimes also upon plants, such as wine. And they have taken for this as their foundation <note>original: *pro fundamento*</note> the words of the **Philosophers**, when they say: "Our matter is everywhere, it is universal, and the poor man has it as well as the rich." Therefore, they have believed that the Master's **Stone of the Wise** <note>The Philosopher's Stone</note> could be made from any thing, and for that very reason there are so many sophisti- <note>The text breaks here with the catchword "sophisti-", likely leading to "sophistis," referring to "sophists" or fraudulent alchemists who deceive others with false processes</note>

<summary>This page introduces the Fourth Letter, written by a member of the Rosicrucian Brotherhood in 1723, warning that many seekers fail because they misunderstand the "universal" nature of the First Matter and waste their wealth on common substances like lead, sulfur, or biological waste.</summary>
<keywords>Lord of Rittersbach, Rosicrucians, First Matter, Alchemical Symbols, Antimony, Gold, Lead, Sulfur, Bismuth, Philosopher's Stone, Sophistry</keywords>

For through dew and rain, this **Fire**-spirit <note>original: *Ignis*</note> sinks into the mountains, where it heats up through the warmth it carries with it and rises here and there within the mountains. Through this **motion** <note>original: *motum*</note> it is brought to an acidic **liquid** <note>original: *liquorem*</note>; this acidic liquid is driven back and forth by its internal (active) **Fire** <note>original: *Ignis*</note> until it finally transforms, with the help of a subtle **viscous water** <note>original: *Aquæ viscosæ*</note>, into a dirty *Gur* <term>Gur: A term used by early modern miners and alchemists to describe a soft, "living" mineral substance or "primal broth" found in rock crevices, believed to be the embryonic form of metals</term>. 

Now, if this *Gur* cannot be driven further by the **motion** <note>original: *motum*</note> of the warmth within it, and thus remains lying there, it finally coagulates into a **native antimony** <note>original: *Antimonium nativo*; the naturally occurring mineral form of antimony</note>. But if such *Gur* is further made volatile by the **Fire** <note>original: *Ignis*</note> of nature, so that it dissolves again, it becomes increasingly acidic, and finally becomes **Air** <note>original: *Aer*</note>. If it dissolves even further, and no foreign impurity is added, it becomes a mass that is very closely related to **Gold**: for it is pure **Fire** <note>original: *Ignis*</note> and **Spirit** <note>original: *Spiritus*</note>; but if this same mass closes itself up, as

Let this spirit stand with its **phlegm** <note>original: Phlegma; the watery, inactive residue left after distillation</note> until I tell you when you should separate it further. This must take place in a large glass, and the aforementioned spirit should be placed in a cool location, well-secured. Distill the "blood" over several times as well, until it becomes shiny and pure. Now take your remaining gray **saltpeter** <term>🜔</term> <note>original: 🜔 (Salniter)</note> and **calcine** <note>to heat a substance to high temperatures to reduce it to a powder or ash</note> it until it is snow-white; afterwards, extract the true **Gold** <term>☉</term> <note>original: ☉ (Sol)</note> or **Antimony** <term>♁</term> <note>original: ♁ (Antimonii)</note> of the Wise from it; keep this **Gold** <term>☉</term> well-secured also. Then distill the **fire** <term>🜂</term> <note>original: 🜂 (Ignis)</note> with the **Eagle** over as well, and repeat the operation seven times, so that they become truly pure. Collect the **phlegm** as well; keep the **fire** <term>🜂</term>, the **sulfur** <term>🜟</term> <note>original: 🜟 (Sulfur)</note>, and the white **Eagle** in a large, strong glass; then take your gold mentioned above and **solve** <note>original: solvire; to dissolve into a liquid state</note> it in the phlegm. Distill this off again, and you must repeat this dissolving and distilling until all the **saltpeter** <term>🜔</term> is removed from the **Gold** <term>☉</term> and the latter remains as an **oil**, which will not return to a solid **gold** <term>☉</term> state in either heat or cold; then it is ready. Put the phlegm aside. Furthermore, I tell you that the **blood of the Lion** is pure air <note>original: Aer</note> and of a fierce nature, and also contains the greatest life force in a hidden way...

## Fifth Letter.

In the year 1723, the following was given to me for practical work by von Vulfstein, a member of the **Fraternity of the Rosy Cross** <note>original: Fraternitatis Roseae Crucis; a secret society of mystics and alchemists</note>:

My dearest brother, I wish to give you here, in a few words, clear instruction according to the teaching of **Brother Basil** <note>referring to Basil Valentine, a legendary 15th-century alchemist whose identity is often debated</note>, when he speaks of the root of the Masters’ **Philosopher’s Stone** in this way:

> A stone is found, it truly is not dear,
> From it a most powerful fire is brought near.

Here he means to say that one could very well obtain this stone, for it is not expensive nor is it difficult to acquire. If one only goes to the miners and asks for the golden <term>Saturno</term> <note>original symbol: ♄no. While ♄ represents Saturn or Lead, in this context it refers to "Saturnine" minerals like stibnite or antimony ore, which were essential to Valentine's work</note> as their "honest fellow," with which they strike fire in the mines, there will be few miners who would not know of it. Furthermore, Basil continues:

> And yet the stone is made from this fire.

<summary>This page introduces the "Fifth Letter," a 1723 Rosicrucian instructional text citing Basil Valentine regarding the "root" of the Philosopher's Stone. It identifies the primary raw material as a common, inexpensive mineral used by miners to strike sparks, identified here as a form of "golden Saturn" or antimony.</summary>
<keywords>Rosicrucians, Basil Valentine, Fifth Letter, Saturno, miners, stibnite, antimony, Philosopher's Stone, 1723</keywords>

From it springs forth a little fountain, bright and clear; that is, dear brother, when you take our **matter** and bring it into an inconceivable powder, bring this to the <term>fire</term> <note>original symbol: $\Delta$</note>, so that our <term>Saturn</term> <note>original symbol: $\hbar$; likely referring here to antimony or the "lead" of the philosophers</note> may be impregnated by it. Yet it does not come all at once; you must often bring your matter back to the air so that it may become living again. Thereupon, give the distilled <term>spirit</term> back to it; in such a way you will significantly come to the aid of your matter, for when you repeat this often, one <term>spirit</term> <note>original symbol: $\sim$</note> sets the other free from its bonds, so that in the end you do not only drive out a <term>spirit</term> <note>original symbol: $\sim$</note> like a clear little fountain from it

## Sixth Letter.

In the year 1724, the following was communicated to me by Nathan from the Netherlands, as a brother of the <term>Fraternity of the Rosy Cross</term> <note>original: Fraternitatis Roseae crucis; a legendary secret society of alchemists and mystics</note>, concerning the manipulation of the great secret work:

My dear brother, since you desire from me an opening regarding the <term>Universal</term> <note>original: Universali; the "Universal Medicine" or Philosopher's Stone capable of curing all diseases and transmuting metals</note> and its medicine: you should know that the Most High has chosen for this purpose only a single <term>matter</term> <note>original: Materie; the raw starting material for alchemy</note>, which is despised by everyone. For what is despised in the eyes of men, that the Most High has chosen; it is even so with our matter, of which an old, experienced philosopher says: there is a single being; from it make two, and make them fly by artifice <note>A reference to the alchemical process of sublimation, where a solid is heated into a vapor ("flying") and then condensed</note>. Our matter, if you know it—and I shall also name it to you—compares itself to a <term>center</term> <note>original: centro</note>, wherefore the philosophers say: the center of this world pushes this seed out from itself as a delicate vapor; the <term>Air</term> <note>original symbol: 🜁</note> is the <term>Fire</term> <note>original symbol: 🜂</note> hidden within our matter. Thus is our <term>Mercurial Fire</term> <note>original symbols: ☿ 🜂; referring to the "philosophical mercury" or the living spirit within the matter</note> mixed with <term>Water</term> <note>original symbol: 🜄</note>; it is a watery fire that scorches the metals; it is a pure spirit,

<summary>This page introduces the Sixth Letter, featuring a 1724 communication from a Rosicrucian named Nathan regarding the "Universal Medicine." It describes the "First Matter" as a humble, despised substance that contains the hidden elemental forces of fire and air.</summary>
<keywords>Sixth Letter, Nathan from the Netherlands, Rosy Cross, Universal Medicine, First Matter, Mercurial Fire, sublimation, 1724</keywords>

Evaporation—in this way, the volatile shall be bound, and when it has stood for its required time, it will pass through wonderful colors and finally become fixed. Therefore, tear your books apart, so that you do not have to tear your hearts apart through grief and worry.

## Second Repetition <note>original: Secunda Repetitio</note>

When you have now putrefied <note>original: putrificiret</note> our matter (for in putrefaction <note>original: Putrefaction</note> also lies the solution <note>original: Solution</note>), there follows the distillation <note>original: Destillation</note>, through which one begins to dissolve, so that spirit, soul, and body separate from our clods of earth; but do not throw these away, but rather purify them through their own spirit, for they lighten your labor. This is to say that through the repetition of the distillation, the spirit and soul rise up; then one takes the <term>water</term> <note>original symbol: ▽</note> and makes from it a <term>salt</term> <note>original symbol: ⊖</note>, as mentioned before. However, the calcination <note>original: Calcination; the process of heating a substance to high temperatures to reduce it to a powder</note> **Note Well** <note>original: NB</note> cannot happen with any benefit if one has not previously dissolved the body correctly.

> But do not burn the bodies to a dead lime,
> Or else the noble talc of the wise would be spoiled.
<note>"Dead lime" refers to material that has been over-burned until it loses its "life" or reactivity. "Talc" in this context refers to the "Talc of the Sages," a symbol for the pure, white, or crystalline state of the alchemical matter.</note>

## Seventh Letter.

->In the year 1724, the following was given to me by Reinhart,<-

This happens when our lame—I might even say our smoking—worm is distilled over in a gentle heat by *cohobating* <note>original: *cohobando*; a process of repeated distillation where the liquid is poured back onto the solid residue to concentrate it</note>, following the steaming *putrefaction* <note>original: *Putrefaction*; the process of breaking down or "rotting" the matter to release its essence</note>; then the white gluten <note>a sticky or viscous substance</note>, whose mother is the Moon, rises over; after that follows the blood of the Red Lion <note>a symbol for a purified, potent form of matter, often associated with gold or antimony</note>. Furthermore, through philosophical *calcination* <note>original: *Calcination*; heating a substance to a high temperature to reduce it to powder</note>, its father, or the Sun, comes to light.

Accordingly, you have the true masterpiece: 🜔 Salt, 🜍 Sulfur, and ☿ Mercury <note>original: *🜔 🜍 et ☿*</note>, or Spirit, Soul, and Body. When all three of these are properly purified, some have taken the △ Fire <note>original: *△*</note> or the rose-red blood and united it with the volatile spirit or gluten, and they have called it their *Hermaphrodite* <note>original: *Hermaphrodit*; an alchemical symbol representing a union of opposites, such as male and female or fixed and volatile</note>; and indeed it is so. In this way, they have prepared the "above" and the "below" <note>original: *superius et inferius*</note>, and afterwards they united the uppermost with the lowermost, bringing them through gentle boiling to the point of *fixation* <note>original: *Fixation*; the process of making a volatile substance stable so it no longer evaporates when heated</note>. Thus you have our Fire and our Sulfur <note>original: *🜍*</note>, for this fire is locked within our sulfur, with which you can perform wonders in nature.

Once you have fully cooked our Stone in the subsequent work, then feed it with Red and White, and continue to boil further; thus you will finally obtain the greatest treasure on earth. From all this, the brother <note>a term of address for a fellow member of an alchemical or Rosicrucian order</note> sees that nothing else is fit for our work. Therefore trust in God, follow Him...

# Eighth Letter.

In the year 1724, I received from von Rantzau, as a member of the <term>Society of the Rosy and Golden Cross</term> <note>original: *societatis Roseae et aureae crucis*; a legendary secret brotherhood of alchemists and mystics who claimed to possess secret wisdom of nature</note>, the following to be
->manipulated.<-

Firstly, the Brother should know what he intends to make; secondly, what effects it should produce; and lastly, what he has then accomplished. For you should know that within our unique <term>Subject</term> <note>original: *Subjecto*; the "Prima Materia" or the raw, base substance chosen by the alchemist to begin the Great Work</note> lie the powers and properties of all stars, animals, herbs, minerals, and metals, as well as all precious stones—indeed, all brilliance and colors—all of which are brought to light out of this dark matter by means of <term>Vulcan</term> <note>original: *Vulcanum*; the Roman god of fire, used here to represent the alchemical fire of the furnace</note>. This living light and <term>Fire</term> <note>original symbol: ∆</note>, with which the heavens, stars, and other elements are thoroughly filled by the Creator, descends into our Subject; firstly when it is born, and secondly when the artist takes it from its dark pit and brings it into the air. In this way, one <term>spirit of air and light</term> attracts the other and multiplies within the matter, which is subsequently called a Balsam of Nature, the <term>Mercury of Life</term> <note>original symbol: ☿; the vital, fluidic essence that sustains growth and transformation</note>, us

# Means to unite the <term>Sulfur</term> <note>the active, "soul" principle; original symbol: 🜍</note> with the <term>Salt</term> <note>the physical "body" or base; original symbol: ⊖</note>, <note>original: "sapienti sat"; a Latin phrase meaning "enough for the wise," used to signal a secret for those initiated in the art</note>.

But as to why the <term>Mercury</term> <note>the volatile "spirit"; original symbol: ☿</note> is as viscous as the <term>Sulfur</term>, I will first give you the reason: it is because it contains all three—the spirit of <term>Mercury</term>, <term>Sulfur</term>, and <term>Salt</term>—within itself. It also, so to speak, keeps the subtle **earth** <note>original: "terram"</note> and its parts unlocked within it. Therefore, it becomes viscous and oily, for it carries such things over with it during the process of **cohobation** <note>the repeated distillation of a liquid from its own residue to concentrate its power</note>.

Then follows, as said before, the sulfurous <term>Sulfur</term> as a companion. But as for our <term>Salt</term>, which is meant to **coagulate** <note>solidify or thicken</note> the two previous **principles** <note>the fundamental components: Mercury and Sulfur</note> into an incorruptible substance that shall make the <term>Mercury</term> and <term>Sulfur</term> living; this is the beginning of our secret. For the <term>Sulfur</term> stirs up all **putrefaction** <note>the essential stage of decay and breakdown that precedes transformation</note> and change within the fixed <term>Salt</term> by warming the innermost part of our central <term>Salt</term>, from which all **corruptions** and **mutations** <note>breakdowns and transformations</note> in the work arise; this is truly the greatest secret of all. 

You must, however, dear brother, know how to purify these well, for our beginnings are at first mixed with many impurities, especially our <term>Sulfur</term>, which has many black **dregs** <note>original: "feces"</note> in and about it. The <term>Mercury</term> must also be purified of its internal **watery** <note>original symbol: 🜄</note> parts, for otherwise it could no longer be effective; likewise, the <term>Salt</term> has a burning **corrosive** with it, which through **dissolving**, **coagulating**, and gentle **calcining** <note>the process of heating a substance to high temperatures to drive off volatile matter or reduce it to ash</note>

## A Repetition.

When you have now impregnated our **matter** <note>the starting physical substance of the work</note> and made it fully living in the **fire** <note>original symbol: △</note>, and have subsequently **putrefied** <note>allowed to decay or break down</note> it in its own **liquor** <note>liquid</note>; then you must well understand that it is exceedingly fluid during its expulsion. For if you were to give a little too much **fire** <note>original symbol: △</note> in the first distillations, everything would melt together, so that it would bind and **fix** <note>solidify into a permanent, non-volatile state</note> itself before the **mercury** <note>original symbol: ☿</note> could even ascend from the matter. Therefore, our distillation must simultaneously be a **circulation** <note>a process where the vapor of a liquid is distilled and then flows back into the same vessel</note> (Note Well), so that the parts that cause the melting are gradually separated. This happens through the **salty** <note>original symbol: ⊖</note> and **sulfurous** <note>original symbol: 🜍</note> **spirits** <note>volatile, refined essences of the substance</note>. For once these are all separated, one no longer needs to fear any particular melting; in the distillation, you obtain two **pure substances** <note>original: "♃ura"; the scribe uses the symbol for Jupiter as shorthand for the letter "P" to write "Pura," meaning pure things</note> from our matter: one is volatile and **mercurial** <note>original symbol: ☿</note>, turning green, while the other is fixed and fiery, which, when

has been made, which happens with its companion, the double Jupiter <note>original symbol: ♃; in alchemy, this refers to tin or a specific purified metallic principle</note>, so that it is driven upward by such cohobation <note>original: cohobando; the alchemical process of repeatedly redistilling a liquid back onto its solid remains to extract the essence</note> and attaches itself like a diamond; then the sages call it Diana <note>a poetic name for the white, lunar stage of the work, often associated with silver or purified mercury</note>. At that point, it receives a true power to transform—that is, when the two have proceeded through the colors until reaching whiteness. With this chaste Diana and with gold, they afterwards went to work and obtained **Note Well** the very greatest mystery <note>original: mysterium; referring to the Great Work or the Philosopher's Stone</note>. Others, however, allowed it to stand longer in the subsequent work until, out of the whiteness, the yellow and finally the highest redness <note>the 'rubedo' or final perfection of the alchemical stone</note> appeared, for which I wish you many blessings. Farewell, Wolfenbüttel, the 22nd of August, 1724.

## Ninth Letter.

In the year 1725, the following was communicated to me for secret manipulation <note>This refers to the practical laboratory procedures or "hand-work" of alchemy</note> by His Excellency the Count of Kreventklaue <note>Possibly a variant of Reventlow, a prominent noble family in the region</note>, as a distinguished member of the *Brotherhood of the Golden and Rosy Cross* <note>original Latin: *Fraternitatis Roseae et Aureae crucis*; a legendary secret society of alchemists and mystics who claimed to possess the secrets of nature and the Philosopher's Stone</note>,

->on the 3rd of March in Helsingør in<-
->Holstein.<-

Since the brother is a member of the *Brotherhood of the Golden and Rosy Cross*, I have no hesitation in providing him with the manual instructions for our secret work. There is good reason to hide this secret from the world rather than to teach it even more clearly. Moreover, the brother knows that the Almighty has threatened the greatest punishment upon anyone who would make this pearl common and, so to speak, cast it before swine <note>A biblical reference to Matthew 7:6, a common trope in alchemical literature used to justify the use of symbols and codes to hide knowledge from the uninitiated</note>. However, since there is nothing to fear here <note>meaning the recipient is already a trusted initiate</note>, I will teach him everything all the more freely, without any scruples of conscience, through emphatic and detailed manual instructions. First of all, however, everything we begin must be in the name 
<meta>catchword: of God</meta>

<summary>This page marks the beginning of the Ninth Letter, written in 1725 by the Count of Kreventklaue, a high-ranking Rosicrucian. It establishes the confidential nature of the alchemical "work" and justifies its secrecy through religious warnings against sharing sacred knowledge with the unworthy.</summary>
<keywords>Rosicrucians, Brotherhood of the Golden and Rosy Cross, Count von Kreventklaue, Helsingør, alchemical secrecy, laboratory manipulation, initiation</keywords>

God’s work, that He may give His blessing to it. Now, when you have reduced our hard body <note>original: *corpus*; the raw material of the alchemical work</note> to an imperceptible powder, then bring it first to the fire <note>represented by $\triangle$ in the original</note>, so that our Elder <note>referring to the "Old Man" or the base state of the prime matter</note> might be refreshed and increase its life from the fire; for our body has many impure dregs <note>original: *excrementa*</note> within it, which keep it covered and imprisoned. When one has set its innermost water <note>represented by $\nabla$ in the original</note> into motion through our putrefaction <note>the process of breaking down a substance through moisture and gentle heat, often symbolized by decay</note>, it is finally dissolved, altered, and at the same time we also complete a minor calcination <note>the reduction of a solid to a powder or ash through heat</note>. Our living water must gradually unlock its own body in this way: for our water is very fiery <note>represented by $\triangle$rig</note>, and is also called fire by the Sages. Therefore, we must perform everything with a very gentle and simple fire; through strong fire, everything is destroyed. This subtle spirit, full of air <note>represented by 🜁er</note>, cannot endure strong fire at all, or it flies away prematurely from our hard body, which has not yet been refined <note>original: *attenuiert*; thinned or made subtle</note>, and the body remains lying undissolved. This does not happen, however, if it is dissolved and gradually unlocked through preceding putrefaction and cohobation <note>the process of repeatedly distilling a liquid over its own solid residue to concentrate its "virtue"</note>; instead, it rises together with its spirit in the form of a clear and heavy water; therefore, two types of operations must take place during the process: first, that through clever labor our Mercury <note>represented by ☿</note> and Sulfur <note>represented by 🜍</note> are gradually separated from their body; second, that one coagulates <note>solidifies or thickens</note> these two as spirit and soul, that is,

# Tenth Letter.

In the year 1725, I received the following for the manipulation <note>the practical laboratory work of alchemy</note> from a fellow brother of the <term>Fraternity of the Rosy Cross</term> <note>original: Fraternitatis Roseae crucis; a legendary secret society of mystics and alchemists</note> by the name of Tuffetang.

The philosophers have hidden our <term>raw matter</term> <note>original: Materiam crudam; the starting substance for the Philosopher's Stone</note> under many strange names; at one time it was called their House, then their <term>magnesia</term> <note>in historical alchemy, "magnesia" was a broad term for various mineral substances believed to contain the "seed" of metals</note>, or their <term>immature electrum</term> <note>original: electrum immaturum; typically a natural alloy of gold and silver</note>. It is, however, known to the brother. The farmer <note>likely a reference to a specific alchemical authority or a metaphorical "simple" observer of nature</note> says of it: this mineral root must be gradually separated from its base nature, which it acquired from poisonous vapors, and then the white <term>mercurial</term> <note>indicated by the symbol ☿</note> lily-juice must be pressed from it. For this is very delicate and volatile, which is why one must seek it in its upper portion: its name is

Now, the ancients had various ways for the **conjunction** <note>original Latin: *conjunctione*; the stage where the volatile spirit and the fixed body are united</note> of these two. Some shortened their work thus: they took 12 **Loth** <note>an old German unit of weight, roughly equivalent to half an ounce or 15 grams</note> of the **laton**, but 24 **Loth** of the **spirit of mercury** <note>original symbols: ∽☿ ii</note> and **circulated** <term>circulated: a process of continuous distillation where the vapor condenses and flows back into the same vessel, often compared to the circulation of blood</term> these two together; they also distilled them by **cohobating** <term>cohobating: the process of pouring a distilled liquid back onto the solid matter from which it was drawn and re-distilling it to further concentrate the "spirit"</term> them over, so that finally they had the **laton** with the **spirit of mercury** <note>original symbols: ∽☿ ii</note> [transformed] into a white **sublimated salt** <note>original symbol: ⊖at</note>, which the philosophers called their **foliated earth** <note>original: *geblätterte* ∇ (earth symbol); also known as *Terra Foliata*, a stage where the matter appears as thin, leaf-like scales</note>. ...more magnificent, which you shall sink into pure moonlight; then the Moon will borrow 6 loth <term>loth: a historical German unit of weight, roughly equivalent to half an ounce or 15 grams</term> from the Sun, and give and impart the same to you for nourishment <note>In this context, "nourishment" refers to financial subsistence or profit from the alchemical work</note>; calculate it yourself: even a great lord, if the work is pursued vigorously, could support himself, and N.B. <note>abbreviation for the Latin "nota bene," meaning "take special note"</note> it costs neither much effort nor money.

This means when you [work] with stones of fire <note>original symbol: $\triangle$</note>—that is, when you have crushed our matter small and gently calcined <term>calcination: the process of heating a substance to high temperatures in a crucible to reduce it to a powder or "ash"</term> it with a mild fire <note>original symbol: $\triangle$</note> under a muffle <note>a ceramic enclosure used in a furnace to protect the material from direct contact with the fuel</note>, or also in a crucible, for so long until the grey smock has vanished. Then, extract the result of the calcination with a solvent <note>original: menstruo</note> which you have made from spirit of vitriol <note>original symbol: $\sim$ 🜖tri; a concentrated sulfuric acid</note> and salmiak <note>original symbol: 🜹; ammonium chloride</note> in equal parts <note>original: ana</note>.

Or, prepare a water <note>original symbol: 🜄</note> of gold <note>original symbol: ☉</note> and silver <note>original symbol: ☽</note> in equal parts <note>original: āā</note>; of this, one takes 1 pound <note>original symbol: ℔</note> and of a strong salmiak <note>original symbol: 🜹</note> also 1 pound. This solvent is just as good as the previous one. With this, one dissolves the red powder. If it produces a green solution, then the antimony <note>original symbol: ♁</note> is not sufficiently calcined; but if, N.B., it is rightly calcined, the solution will become red. In this latter case, one extracts everything, and when no more of the redness can be perceived, one pours off all the extractions and distills everything from it until only a red powder remains. You may wash <note>original: absüssen; literally "sweeten," meaning to wash a precipitate with water to remove salts</note> this a few more times, and herewith it is prepared.

## Eleventh Letter.

In the year 1725, I received from Father
Schwarz of the <term>Society of Jesus <note>original Latin: societate Iesu; the Jesuit order</note></term> as a <term>missionary <note>original Latin: Missionario</note></term>

the following for manipulation: Maastricht,
->the 8th of August.<-

Because we are bound to one another to
show the way to truth, and it is also
Christian for one brother to serve another:
I would have preferred to describe this
secret to you in the Latin language, because such—

[...extract] it into various well-secured vessels, place it in a moist, steaming heat for one month; then, through putrefaction <note>The process of decomposition or rotting, which alchemists believed was necessary to break down the "body" of a substance and release its internal "spirit."</note>, one spirit will release and open the other. At that point, distill its spirit over in a very gentle heat, and pour it back over fresh matter that has also been putrefied. Your first distilled subject <note>Latin: *subjectum*; the base material or "subject" of the work.</note> should be brought back to the air to be revived, for it is highly magnetic and draws the <term>air</term> and the spirit of the world <note>The *Spiritus Mundi*, a hypothetical universal life force that alchemists believed could be harvested from the atmosphere, particularly in spring.</note> to itself.

When the spirit is then distilled off again, pour it onto new, fresh matter. If, however, you have no more that has been putrefied, take that which has been impregnated *anew* <note>Latin: *de novo*</note> from the <term>air</term> and pour the liquid <note>Latin: *liquorem*</note> upon it. Then, bring everything together and let the liquid <note>Latin: *liquorem*</note> stand well-sealed in gentle heat, so that all impurities settle to the bottom. ## Twelfth Letter.

In the year 1725, the following procedure was given to me by J. D. Weise, as a member of the <term>Rosy Cross</term> <note>original: membro Roseae Crucis; refers to the Rosicrucians, a secret brotherhood of alchemists and mystics.</note>,

This matter of ours does not require great expense, for it is separated by the help of Nature and the artist's clever techniques so that, as Theophrastus says, it is transmuted into the <term>White Eagle</term> <note>original: weissen Adler; often represents the volatile principle, such as purified mercury or sal ammoniac.</note> and the Red Lion; moreover, the radiance of the <term>Sun</term> <note>original: solis; refers to gold or the solar principle.</note> should not merely shine for the <term>spagyrist</term> <note>original: Spagyro; a practitioner of spagyrics, the branch of alchemy involving the separation and recombination of plant or mineral components.</note> after the work is done, but rather the matter must show such radiance beforehand, according to the external <term>signature</term> <note>original: Signatur; refers to the "Doctrine of Signatures," the belief that the outward appearance of a substance reveals its inner medicinal or spiritual properties.</note> of the Sun’s brilliance, which is well to note; and although our

The <term>Subject</term> <note>original: Subjectum; the raw, starting material of the alchemical process.</note> must be fetched out of a black, dark dwelling; then it is, in its form, black on the outside and also appears as if dusted with **Sun** <note>original symbol: $\odot$; refers to gold or the solar principle.</note>, but in its innermost part it is a purely volatile **Sun** ($\odot$) seed. This is also indicated by the external shape and weight of the matter, and that it shall be transformed into the <term>White Eagle</term> <note>A symbol for the volatile, purified state of the matter, often associated with mercury.</note> and the <term>Red Lion</term> <note>A symbol for the fixed, sulfurous principle or the final stage of the work.</note> through the help of Nature. Then, as **Basilius Valentinus** <note>Basil Valentine, a legendary 15th-century alchemist and monk.</note> says, a spirit is prepared from it, white as snow, and another spirit, red as blood; which two spirits then still have the third hidden within them. And **Richardus Anglus** <note>Richard the Englishman, a 13th-century alchemical writer.</note> says: there is also a medicine prepared from a stone, which is called **Rebis** <note>From the Latin "res bina," meaning "double thing"; it represents the divine hermaphrodite or the union of spirit and body.</note> by the philosophers and consists of two things, namely of body and spirit, white and red. These two things are but a single one, especially because of the **Water** <note>original symbol: $\nabla$.</note> that we add to the body, dissolving it into a spirit; that is, bringing it into a double **essential** <note>original: $\mathfrak{E}$ial; an abbreviation for "essentialis."</note> **Water** ($\nabla$), from which it was initially made, and thus a mineral **Water** ($\nabla$) is made from the body and spirit; then the **Water** ($\nabla$) and the body are one thing. Therefore, **Bernhard, Count of Trevisan** <note>Bernhardus Graf von Tresvis (1406–1490), an influential Italian alchemist.</note> and the March says thus: Our work is made in the beginning from one root, that is, from a sin-

## Thirteenth Letter.

In the year 1726, the following was communicated to me for the laboratory procedure by Brother Guardian Lothario of the Franciscan Order, as a member of the **Rose and Golden Cross** <note>original: *Roseae et Aureae Crucis*; a secret fraternal order of alchemists and mystics who claimed to possess ancient wisdom.</note>: Brussels, the 6th of April.

Because my duty requires me to serve my brother, I have wished to do so here with simple rather than high-flown words, briefly and without any deception in the truth, for I hold no stock in excessive philosophizing. Regarding our **Matter** <note>original: *Materie*; the "Prima Materia" or starting substance required to create the Philosopher's Stone.</note> first of all: it is found everywhere, and if someone understands **mines** <note>original: *Bergwerke*.</note> even slightly—how metals and minerals are generated within them—he can easily find it. The Philosophers say that neither metals nor minerals are suitable for our work, and yet it is said to be a mineral; however, it does not belong to the common ones. Therefore, many have settled upon **Antimony** <term>♁</term> <note>The symbol ♁ refers to Antimony, a metalloid frequently used in alchemical purification processes and often considered a "child" of the primary matter.</note> because it is designated as such by most, and is at the same time a product of our Matter. **Pontanus** <note>Johannes Pontanus was a famous alchemist who claimed to have traveled the world and failed at alchemy until he discovered the "secret fire" mentioned in his well-known "Epistle."</note> stated: one should take **Antimony** <term>♁</term>

<summary>This letter, dated 1726, introduces a communication from a member of the Rosicrucian Order concerning the identity of the "First Matter." It suggests that while many mistake common Antimony for the secret ingredient, the true substance is a related mineral found in mines.</summary>
<keywords>Brother Lothario, Rose and Golden Cross, First Matter, mining, Antimony (♁), Pontanus, Rosicrucianism, 1726</keywords>

If you now know our matter—as it should well be known to you—see to it that you obtain a sizeable supply of it, for the Pure is hidden within it in very small amounts, and know that you must open its body through very clever manual operations. Many get this matter into their hands, yet they get nothing from it, for it is difficult to extract **Sulfurs** <term>🜍-ers</term> from this hard rock. ## The Subsequent Work.

Now the ancients had two paths before them: a long one, which they called the **wet way** <note>original: *nassen Weg*</note>, for which one must have a year and a day for fixation <term>fixation</term> <note>original: *ad fixationem*; the process of making a volatile substance stable so it no longer evaporates when heated</note> before enjoying the fruit thereof; this requires great patience and constant boiling, which proceeds as follows.

Take 5 parts of our fixed body, which the philosophers call *laton* <term>laton</term> <note>original: *latonem*; a term for the "base" or "black" metal that must be purified and whitened</note>, and 2 or even 3 parts of the fatty liquor <term>liquor</term> <note>original: *liquore*</note>; bring these together into a vial, hermetically sealed <term>hermetically sealed</term> <note>original: *hermetice sigilliret*; an airtight seal made by melting the glass neck of a flask</note>, placed in our secret furnace <term>Athanor</term> <note>original: *Athanor*; a specialized alchemical furnace designed to provide steady, prolonged heat</note>, and let it stand in very gentle heat. Thus it begins to putrefy <term>putrefy</term> <note>original: *faulen*; the stage of decomposition, also known as the *nigredo* or "blackness," essential for rebirth in the work</note> once more and, after a month, becomes pitch black. When it begins to turn white, one gradually increases the heat to the second degree, until it becomes white, for also—

the 3rd degree [of heat], and when it has thoroughly turned yellow, then one also gives it the 4th degree and lets it stand in such heat until it has become red; then one takes the tincture <term>tincture</term> <note>The final transformative substance sought in alchemy</note> out, grinds it small, and gives it again 2 loth <term>loth</term> <note>A historical German unit of weight, roughly 14.5 grams or half an ounce</note> of the fat water <term>Water</term> <note>original symbol: 🜄; likely referring to the concentrated mercurial liquid mentioned on the previous page</note>, seals it again hermetically <note>original: *hermetice*; an airtight seal, named after Hermes Trismegistus, using melted glass or wax</note>, and lets it first pass through the colors, which is brought through the colors faster the second time than it was the first time, and this happens with every imbibition <term>imbibition</term> <note>The process of "feeding" the dry alchemical matter with liquid until it is saturated</note>, so that it is finally completed in a few weeks. ---

## The Other and Short Way in the Follow-up Work.

Take 6 loth of our fixed salamander <term>salamander</term> <note>In alchemy, the "salamander" is a symbol for a substance that can endure the fire without being consumed—the "fixed" matter</note>, pour onto it in a flask 12 loth of our white gluten or mercury <term>mercury</term> <note>original symbol: ☿io; likely referring to a specific mercurial solvent or "secret" liquid</note>, seal the flask with a blind helm <note>A still-head without a spout or beak, used to force vapors to condense and fall back down (reflux) rather than exit the vessel</note>, and let it circulate together for 14 days and nights <note>original: *14 d. n.* (dies et noctes)</note>. Then begin to distill and to cohobate <term>cohobate</term> <note>The process of repeatedly distilling a liquid and pouring it back onto the solid residue remaining in the vessel</note> several times, at the last always giving a little strong fire <term>fire</term> <note>original symbol: △</note>, until you see that all the fixed part settles as a white, shining sublimate <term>sublimate</term> <note>original: ♁at; the solid substance produced when a material is heated into vapor and then solidifies again</note> and has sublimated <note>original: auf♁iret</note> up from its water <term>water</term> <note>original symbol: ∇</note>; then separate this noble sublimate from the dregs <note>original: *Fra*, likely a shorthand for *faeces* or dross</note> and bring it to that which is hidden above in the helm, and sublimate <note>original: ♁re</note> it once more; thus you have what the philosophers call their "chaste Diana" <note>original: *Dianam*; a mythological reference to the moon, symbolizing the white, silver-like stage of the Great Work</note> or "Doves of Hermes" <note>original: *Columbas Hermetis*; a poetic name for the white, volatile vapors that rise and settle in the vessel</note>; preserve them together, and take from your fixed salamander, which the philosophers also call laton <note>original: *latonem*; a term for the "impure" base metal or "bronze" that must be cleansed to reach the white stage</note>...

<summary>This page describes the extraordinary potency of the alchemical tincture, which can transform massive quantities of base metal into gold, and introduces a "Short Way" to produce the "Chaste Diana" or white sublimate.</summary>

Introduced *Saturn* <note>Lead.</note>,
And also *Mars*, the vigorous one <note>Iron.</note>,
Is *transformed* <note>original: "tingirt." To "tinge" is the alchemical act of changing the color and nature of a base metal into gold.</note> to gold through you.

->9.<-

*Shadow* <note>original: "Umbra."</note> was first your name,
Miner’s flintstone,

<summary>This page continues the alchemical hymn, describing a universal solvent (Azoth) and the paradoxical "dry water" that purifies and transforms metals. It lists the various names of the Philosophers' Stone and its ability to turn base metals like lead and iron into gold.</summary>
<keywords>Azoth, Rebis, Laton, Mercury of the Sages, transmutation, Carbuncle Stone, dry water, wet fire, Sol, Luna, Saturn, Mars</keywords>

--- Page 142 ---
<meta>This page concludes the alchemical hymn begun on the previous pages. It completes stanza nine and provides the final stanza, number ten. The imagery shifts from the names of the substance to the physical signs (glimmer and scent) that miners and alchemists look for in the earth.</meta>

<page-num>120</page-num>
<header><unclear>scribbled out text</unclear></header>

The seed of gold and silver <note>original: "Gold und Silber = Saame." Alchemists believed metals grew from seeds within the earth, much like plants.</note>
Are not common within you,
The miners do indeed name you,
As do other children of men,
Yet they do not know your scent <note>original: "Wittrung." A mining term referring to the "exhalation" or atmospheric sign that an ore vein is nearby.</note>
And are thereby stark blind.

->10.<-

They certainly see your glimmer,
Yet they do not value you as pyrite <note>original: "Kieß." Often referring to iron pyrites or "fool's gold," which was frequently discarded despite its metallic shine.</note>,
Even though your beautiful shimmer
Shows the light even to the half-blind.
Blessed is he who works you!
Together with the base <note>original: "schlecht." In this period, "schlecht" often meant "simple," "plain," or "base" rather than "bad."</note> mother of all,
From which a Stone is prepared,
He truly possesses a work of gold.

->The End.<-

<summary>This page concludes an alchemical poem describing the hidden nature of the "seed" of precious metals and the inability of common miners to recognize the true transformative substance. It ends by praising the alchemist who can prepare the Philosophers' Stone from these seemingly base materials.</summary>
<keywords>alchemy, mining, seed of metals, Wittrung, pyrite, Philosophers' Stone, spiritual blindness</keywords>

--- Page 148 ---
<meta>This page moves away from the poetic text of the previous pages to describe the physical exterior of the manuscript. The previous page (120) concluded the alchemical hymn regarding the "seed of gold." This page documents the book's binding and craftsmanship.</meta>

### Manuscript Binding

The volume is bound in dark brown leather, featuring decorative elements created through blind-tooling <term>blind-tooling: a technique where heated metal tools are pressed into leather to create indented patterns without the use of gold leaf or ink</term>. 

The design consists of:
*   Two concentric rectangular borders formed by simple, clean lines.
*   A large, vertical, almond-shaped <term>mandorla: an Italian word for "almond," referring to the decorative frame in the center</term> ornament positioned in the middle of the cover.
*   The central ornament contains symmetrical foliate <note>leaf-shaped</note> scrollwork.
*   Four circular motifs featuring stylized floral or star-like designs.

The leather displays characteristic signs of age and wear. Along the right edge, the spine is visible, constructed with raised bands <note>the horizontal ridges on a book's spine where the cords used to sew the pages are covered by leather</note>.

---

**Terminology for this Page:**
<term>binding</term>, <term>blind-tooling</term>, <term>leather</term>, <term>foliate ornament</term>, <term>mandorla</term>, <term>scrollwork</term>

<summary>This page provides a detailed description of the manuscript's physical exterior, specifically its aged leather binding and the traditional blind-tooled decorative motifs including a central mandorla.</summary>
<keywords>bookbinding, blind-tooling, leather, mandorla, foliate design, manuscript conservation</keywords>
